and
t=W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{e}}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Then:
W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}u^{\prime}]
W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u^{\prime}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}u^{\prime}]
W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}u^{\prime}]
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{e}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{e}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}

3.

Let
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}
and
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}}t^{\prime\prime}
. By induction on
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}
:

•

Root
, that is,
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle s\rangle
with
x\notin\mathtt{fv}(s)
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}}t^{\prime\prime}
:

(a)

Root
: impossible because
t
is not an application.

(b)

Step in
s
, that is,
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}}s^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]=t^{\prime\prime}
because
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}}s^{\prime}
. Then:
s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]
S\langle s\rangle
s^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]
S\langle s\rangle^{\prime}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}

(c)

Step in
S
, that is,
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle]=t^{\prime\prime}
. Then:
s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]
S\langle s\rangle
s[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle]
S^{\prime}\langle s\rangle
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}

•

Left of an application
, that is,
t=su\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}u=t^{\prime}
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}}t^{\prime\prime}
:

(a)

Step in
s
. Then it follows from the
i.h.

(b)

Step in
u
. Then the two steps are disjoint and trivially swap.

(c)

Step at the root
, that is,
s=S\langle\lambda y.r\rangle
. Then
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
takes place in
S
. The diagram closes as follows:
S\langle\lambda y.r\rangle s
S^{\prime}\langle\lambda y.r\rangle s
S\langle r[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\rangle
S^{\prime}\langle r[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\rangle
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}

•

Right of an application
, that is,
t=us\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}us^{\prime}=t^{\prime}
. Analogous to the previous case.

(a)

Step in
s
. Then it follows from the
i.h.

(b)

Step in
u
. Then the two steps are disjoint and trivially swap.

(c)

Step at the root
, that is,
u=S\langle\lambda y.r\rangle
. Then the diagram closes as follows:
S\langle\lambda y.r\rangle s
S\langle\lambda y.r\rangle s^{\prime}
S\langle r[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\rangle
S\langle r[y{\shortleftarrow}s^{\prime}]\rangle
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}

•

Left of a substitution
, that is,
t=s[y{\shortleftarrow}u]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]=t^{\prime}
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}}t^{\prime\prime}
:

(a)

Step in
s
. Then it follows from the
i.h.

(b)

Step in
u
. Then the two steps are disjoint and trivially swap.

•

Right of a substitution
, that is,
t=u[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}u[y{\shortleftarrow}s^{\prime}]=t^{\prime}
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}}t^{\prime\prime}
:

(a)

Step in
s
. Then it follows from the
i.h.

(b)

Step in
u
. Then the two steps are disjoint and trivially swap.∎

Appendix B
Proofs of Sect.
5
(Silly Multi Types)

\gettoappendix

l:typctx-varocc-tm

Proof.

Straightforward induction on
\pi
. Note that, if one excludes rule
\lambda
, the management of type contexts by the rules of the type systems mimics exactly the definition of
\mathtt{shfv}(t)
, in particular in rules
@
and
\mathsf{ES}
one can alway apply the
i.h.
to the right premise because of the
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
requirement, which forbids rule
\mathsf{many}
with zero premises. Rule
\lambda
is what might allow
\mathtt{shfv}(t)\subsetneq\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}})
, as one might also type under abstraction, thus extending
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}})
beyond
\mathtt{shfv}(t)
. The part
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}})\subseteq\mathtt{fv}(t)
is due to the absence of weakening in the type system.∎

Lemma 38

(Splitting multi-sets with respect to derivations)
.

Let
t
be a term,
\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}
a derivation, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
a splitting. Then there exist two derivations

•

\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}})}t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}
, and

•

\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}})}t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}

such that:

•

Type contexts
:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}
,

•

Indices
:
m=m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}
and
e=e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}
.

Proof.

The last rule of
\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}
can only be
\mathsf{many}
, thus it is enough to re-group its hypotheses according to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
.∎

Appendix C
Proofs of Sect.
6
(The Weak Calculus, Types, and Strong Normalization)

Lemma 39

(Linear substitution)
.

If
\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m,e)}{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}}

then there is a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
such that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}
with
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})
there is a derivation

\pi^{\prime}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m+m^{\prime},e+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\pi
. Cases:

1.

Linear types
, that is,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
. Cases of
W
:

•

Empty context
, i.e.
W=\langle\cdot\rangle
. The typing derivation
\pi
is simply

{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,1)}{x}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}
is empty. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
is empty. The statement then holds with respect to
\pi^{\prime}:=\rho
, because
m=0
, and
e=1
, which give
m+m^{\prime}=m^{\prime}
and
e+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}|=1+e^{\prime}-1=e^{\prime}
.

•

Left of an application
, that is,
W=W^{\prime}s
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{x\colon({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}});({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+1,e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}
,
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}})\cup\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
,
m=m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+1
, and
e=e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
.

By
i.h.
, there exists a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
such that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}
there exists a derivation:

\pi_{W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}{W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

By applying a
@
rule, we obtain:

{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}{W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{x\colon({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}});({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+1,e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

By defining
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
, we obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
. By applying the equalities on the type context, the last obtained judgement is in fact:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+1,e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

and, by applying those on the indices, we obtain
e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}=e+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|
as required.

•

Right of an application
, that is,
W=sW^{\prime}
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}})}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{x\colon({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}});({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+1,e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}
,
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}})\cup\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
,
m=m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+1
, and
e=e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
.

By
i.h.
, there exists a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
such that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}
there exists a derivation:

\pi_{W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+m^{\prime},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}{W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

By applying a
@
rule, we obtain:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}})}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+m^{\prime},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}{W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

@

{x\colon({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime});({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+m^{\prime}+1,e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}sW^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

By defining
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}
, we obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
. By applying the equalities on the type context, the last obtained judgement is in fact:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+1,e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

and, by applying those on the indices, we obtain
e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}=e+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|
as required.

•

Left of a substitution
, i.e.
W=W^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]
. Note that
x\neq y
, because the hypothesis
W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
implies that
W
does not capture
x
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{x}\,\triangleright{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}};y\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{x\colon({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}});({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}
,
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}})\cup\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
,
m=m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
, and
e=e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
.

Since we are working up to
\alpha
-equivalence, we can assume that
y\notin\mathtt{fv}(t)
, which implies
y\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})
by Lemma
9
.
By
i.h.
, there exists a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}
wtih
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
such that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}
there exists a derivation:

\pi_{W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};y\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}{W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}.

By applying rule
\mathsf{ES}
, we obtain:

{x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};y\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime})}{W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

By defining
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
, we obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
. By applying the equalities on the type context, the last obtained judgement is in fact:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

and, by applying those on the indices, we obtain
e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}=e+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|
as required.

•

Right of a substitution
, that is,
W=s[y{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}]
. Then,
\pi
has the following form:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{s},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{s}}\vdash^{(m_{s},e_{s})}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{W^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W^{\prime}}}\vdash^{(m_{W^{\prime}},e_{W^{\prime}})}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{s}(x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{s}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{W^{\prime}},({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{s}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W^{\prime}}}\vdash^{(m_{s}+m_{W^{\prime}},e_{s}+e_{W^{\prime}})}s[y{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

where

–

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{s}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{W^{\prime}}
,

–

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{s}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W^{\prime}}
,

–

y\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W^{\prime}})
,

–

x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}})
,

–

m=m_{s}+m_{W^{\prime}}
, and

–

e=e_{s}+e_{W^{\prime}}
.

Since we are working up to
\alpha
-equivalence, we can assume that
y\notin\mathtt{fv}(t)
, which implies
y\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})
by Lemma
9
.
By
i.h.
, there exists a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{s}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
such that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}
there exists a derivation:

\rho_{W^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{W^{\prime}}+m^{\prime},e_{W^{\prime}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}{W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{s}(x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

By applying rule
\mathsf{ES}
, we obtain:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{s},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{s}}\vdash^{(m_{s},e_{s})}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\rho_{W^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{s}+m^{\prime},e_{W^{\prime}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}{W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{s}(x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{s}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{s}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m_{s}+m_{W^{\prime}}+m^{\prime},e_{s}+e_{W^{\prime}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}s[y{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

By defining
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{s}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}
, we obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{s}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{W^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{s}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
. By applying the equalities on the type context, the last obtained judgement is in fact:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})}\vdash^{(m_{s}+m^{\prime}+m_{W^{\prime}},e_{s}+e_{W^{\prime}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}s[y{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

and, by applying those on the indices, we obtain
e_{s}+e_{W^{\prime}}+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|=e+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|

as required.

2.

Multi types
, that is,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}
. The last rule is necessarily
\mathsf{many}
and
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{i}\,\triangleright({x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\vdash^{(m_{i},e_{i})}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i})_{{i\in I}}

\mathsf{many}

{x\colon\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{i},\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\vdash^{(\sum_{{i\in I}}m_{i},\sum_{{i\in I}}e_{i})}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{{i\in I}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}=\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{i}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}=\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{{i\in I}}
.
By
i.h.
(point 1) applied to each
\pi_{i}
, there exists a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{i}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
such that for every derivation
\rho_{i}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{i}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime}_{i},e^{\prime}_{i})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}}
there exists derivations:

\pi_{i}^{\prime}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{i};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{i}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{i}+m^{\prime}_{i},e_{i}+e^{\prime}_{i}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}|)}{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}}

Therefore, there exists the following derivation
\pi^{\prime}
:

\pi^{\prime}:=\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 15.802pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 42.24127pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{ $\pi^{\prime}_{i}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{i};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{i}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{i}+m^{\prime}_{i},e_{i}+e^{\prime}_{i}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}|)}{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}}$ }\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.69258pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to311.33516pt{\hrule\hfill}\lower 0.74812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathsf{many}$}}\vskip-0.69257pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{ ${x:\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{i},\uplus_{{i\in I}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{i})}\vdash^{(\sum_{{i\in I}}(m_{i}+m^{\prime}_{i}),\sum_{{i\in I}}(e_{i}+e^{\prime}_{i}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}|))}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{{i\in I}}$ }\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces

By setting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}:=\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}:=\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{i}
, we obtain a splitting of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
. Note that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
follows from the fact that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
.
By applying the equalities on multi types and type contexts, the last obtained judgement is in fact:

{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})}\vdash^{(\sum_{{i\in I}}(m_{i}+m^{\prime}_{i}),\sum_{{i\in I}}(e_{i}+e^{\prime}_{i}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}|))}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}

and, by applying those on the indices, we obtain
\sum_{{i\in I}}(m_{i}+m^{\prime}_{i})=m+m^{\prime}
and
\sum_{{i\in I}}(e_{i}+e^{\prime}_{i}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}|)=e+e^{\prime}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|

as required.
∎

\gettoappendix

prop:open-subject-reduction

Proof.

1.

Sub-points:

(a)

Root step
, that is,
t=S\langle\lambda x.u\rangle r\mapsto_{\mathtt{m}}S\langle u[x{\shortleftarrow}r]\rangle=s
. By induction on
S
. Two sub-cases:

•

Empty substitution context
, i.e.
S=\langle\cdot\rangle
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u},e_{u})}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

\lambda

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u},e_{u})}{\lambda x.u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}(x)\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{r},e_{r})}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}(x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

@

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}+m_{r}+1,e_{u}+e_{r})}{(\lambda x.u)r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}
,
m=m_{u}+m_{r}+1
, and
e=e_{u}+e_{r}
. Note that
m\geq 1
as required. We construct the
following derivation
\rho
, verifying the statement:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u},e_{u})}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{r},e_{r})}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}(x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}+m_{r},e_{u}+e_{r})}{u[x{\shortleftarrow}r]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

•

Non-empty substitution context
, i.e.
S=S^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}p]
. Then
\pi
has the following structure:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u},e_{u})}{S^{\prime}\langle\lambda x.u\rangle}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{p},e_{p})}{p}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}(y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}+m_{p},e_{u}+e_{p})}{S^{\prime}\langle\lambda x.u\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}p]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{r},e_{r})}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}+m_{p}+m_{r}+1,e_{u}+e_{p}+e_{r})}{S^{\prime}\langle\lambda x.u\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}p]r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}
,
m=m_{u}+m_{p}+m_{r}+1
, and
e=e_{u}+e_{p}+e_{r}
. Note that
m\geq 1
as required.

Consider the following derivation, obtained by removing the rule (es):

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u},e_{u})}{S^{\prime}\langle\lambda x.u\rangle}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{r},e_{r})}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}+m_{r}+1,e_{u}+e_{r})}{S^{\prime}\langle\lambda x.u\rangle r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

By
i.h.
, we obtain a derivation

\sigma\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}+m_{r},e_{u}+e_{r})}{S^{\prime}\langle u[x{\shortleftarrow}r]\rangle}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

Now, we apply a rule
\mathsf{ES}
with respect to
y
and
p
, obtaining the following derivation
\rho
, satisfying the statement:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}+m_{r},e_{u}+e_{r})}{S^{\prime}\langle u[x{\shortleftarrow}r]\rangle}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{p},e_{p})}{p}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}+m_{p}+m_{r},e_{u}+e_{p}+e_{r})}{S^{\prime}\langle u[x{\shortleftarrow}r]\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}p]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

(b)

Contextual closure
. We have
t=W\langle u\rangle\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}}W\langle r\rangle=s
. Cases of
W
:

•

Empty
, that is,
t\mapsto_{\mathtt{m}}s
. Then it follows from the previous point.

•

Left of an application
, that is,
W=W^{\prime}p
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{p}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m_{p}+1,e_{u}+e_{p})}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}
,
m=m_{u}+m_{p}+1
, and
e=e_{u}+e_{p}
.

By
i.h.
,
m_{u}\geq 1
and there exists a derivation
\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{u},e_{u})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
with
m>m^{\prime}
, thus allowing us to construct
\rho
as follows:

\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{u},e_{u})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{p}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{u}+m_{p}+1,e_{u}+e_{p})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

Note that
m_{u}+m_{p}+1>m^{\prime}_{u}+m_{p}+1
.

•

Right of an application
, that is,
W=pW^{\prime}
. By setting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{{i\in I}}
and
I=\{1,..,k\}
,
\pi
has the following form:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\vdash^{(m_{i},e_{i})}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i})_{{i\in I}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(m_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathsf{many}

{(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(\sum_{{i\in I}}m_{i}+m_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},\sum_{{i\in I}}e_{i}+e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(m_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}m_{i}+m_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},e_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}e_{i}+e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}pW^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
,
m=m_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}m_{i}+m_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
, and
e=e_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}e_{i}+e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
. By
i.h.
there are derivations of final judgements
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{i},e_{i})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}
with
m_{i}>m^{\prime}_{i}
for
{i\in I}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
with

, so that we obtain the following derivation:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{i},e_{i})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i})_{{i\in I}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathsf{many}

{(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(\sum_{{i\in I}}m^{\prime}_{i}+m^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},\sum_{{i\in I}}e_{i}+e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(m_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}m^{\prime}_{i}+m^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},e_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}e_{i}+e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}pW^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

Note that
m_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}m_{i}+m_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}>m_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}m^{\prime}_{i}+m^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}=m
.

•

Left of a substitution
, that is,
W=W^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}p]
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u},x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{p}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m_{p},e_{u}+e_{p})}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}p]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}
,
m=m_{u}+m_{p}
, and
e=e_{u}+e_{p}
.

By
i.h.
,
m_{u}\geq 1
, and so

, and there exists a derivation
\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u},x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{u},e_{u})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

, thus allowing us to construct
\rho
as follows:

\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u},x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{u},e_{u})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{p}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{u}+m_{p},e_{u}+e_{p})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}p]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

Note that
m_{u}+m_{p}>m^{\prime}_{u}+m_{p}
, as required.

•

Right of a substitution
, that is,
W=p[x{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}]
. By setting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}(x)={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{{i\in I}}
and

,
\pi
has the following form:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\vdash^{(m_{i},e_{i})}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i})_{{i\in I}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(m_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathsf{many}

{(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(\sum_{{i\in I}}m_{i}+m_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},\sum_{{i\in I}}e_{i}+e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}(x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(m_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}m_{i}+m_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},e_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}e_{i}+e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}p[x{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
,
m=m_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}m_{i}+m_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
, and
e=e_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}e_{i}+e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
. By
i.h.
there are derivations of final judgements
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{i},e_{i})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}
with

for

and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
with
