, so that we obtain the following derivation:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{i},e_{i})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i})_{{i\in I}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathsf{many}

{(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(\sum_{{i\in I}}m^{\prime}_{i}+m^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},\sum_{{i\in I}}e_{i}+e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}(x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}\vdash^{(m_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}m^{\prime}_{i}+m^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}},e_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}e_{i}+e_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}})}p[x{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

Note that
m_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}m_{i}+m_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}>m_{p}+\sum_{{i\in I}}m^{\prime}_{i}+m^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}=m
.

2.

Exponential.
Cases:

•

Root step
, i.e.
t=W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\mapsto_{\mathtt{e}_{W}}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Given a derivation
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}^{\prime}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}^{\prime}(x)\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
by Lemma
9
, which can be applied because
x\in\mathtt{shfv}(W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}})
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W},x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash^{(m_{W},e_{W})}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m_{W}+m_{u},e_{W}+e_{u})}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}
,
m=m_{W}+m_{u}
, and
e=e_{W}+e_{u}
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
be the splitting of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}
given by the linear substitution lemma (Lemma
57
) applied to
\pi_{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}
. By the multi-sets splitting lemma (Lemma
38
) applied to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}
, there exist two derivations:

(a)

\rho_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}},e_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}})}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}
and

(b)

\rho_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},e_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}})}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}
.

such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
,
m_{u}=m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}+m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
, and
e_{u}=e_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}+e_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}
.

Now, by applying again the linear substitution lemma to
\pi_{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}
with respect to
\rho_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}
, we obtain a derivation:

\pi_{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{W}+m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}},e_{W}+e_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

Then
\rho
is built as follows:

{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{W}+m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}},e_{W}+e_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},e_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}})}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\vdash^{(m_{W}+m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}+m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},e_{W}+e_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}+e_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

Now, note that the last judgement is in fact

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m_{W}+m_{u},e_{W}+e_{u}-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

which in turn is

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|)}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

as required, in particular
e^{\prime}:=e-|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}|
is strictly smaller than
e
because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
.

•

Contextual closure.
As in the
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}}
case, the only change is that one looks at the
e
index rather than the
m
one. Note that indeed those cases do not depend on the details of the step itself, but only on the context enclosing it.

3.

Garbage collection by value
. Cases:

•

Root step
, i.e.
t=u[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle u\rangle=s
. By induction on
S
. Cases:

(a)

S=\langle\cdot\rangle
. Then
t=u[x{\shortleftarrow}v]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}u=s
with
x\notin\mathtt{fv}(u)
. By Lemma
9
, given a derivation
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
one has
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}(x)={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
. Then, the derivation
\pi
for
t
has the following shape:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\mathsf{ax}_{\lambda}

{}\vdash^{(0,0)}v\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathsf{many}

{}\vdash^{(0,0)}v\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}u[x{\shortleftarrow}v]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

Then
\rho:=\pi_{u}
verifies the statement, since
|\rho|=|\pi|-2
(the
\mathsf{many}
rule does not count for the size).

(b)

S=S^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}r]
. Then
t=u[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S^{\prime}\langle u\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]=s
with
x\notin\mathtt{fv}(u)
. By Lemma
9
, given a derivation
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
one has
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}(x)={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
. Then, the derivation
\pi
for
t
has the following shape:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash^{(m_{r},e_{r})}r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}(y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}+m_{r},e^{\prime}+e_{r})}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathsf{many}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}+m_{r},e^{\prime}+e_{r})}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m^{\prime}+m_{r},e_{u}+e^{\prime}+e_{r})}u[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}
,
m=m_{u}+m^{\prime}+m_{r}
, and
e=e_{u}+e^{\prime}+e_{r}
.
Then consider the following derivation
\pi^{\prime}
, where the substitution
[y{\shortleftarrow}r]
has been removed and of size
|\pi^{\prime}|=|\pi|-1
:

\pi^{\prime}:=\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 25.3053pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{ ${{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}$ }\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{ ${{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}$ }\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.69258pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to93.8393pt{\hrule\hfill}\lower 0.74812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathsf{many}$}}\vskip-0.69257pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{ ${{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}$ }\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to188.55504pt{\hrule\hfill}\lower 2.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathsf{ES}$}}\vskip-0.97035pt\hbox{\quad\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{ ${{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m^{\prime},e_{u}+e^{\prime})}u[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}$ }\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces

By
i.h.
, we obtain a derivation
\rho^{\prime}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m^{\prime},e_{u}+e^{\prime})}S^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
such that
|e|^{\prime}<|\pi^{\prime}|
. Finally, by re-introducing
[y{\shortleftarrow}r]
we obtain the derivation
\rho
satisfying the statement and such that
|\rho|=|\rho^{\prime}|+1<_{{\textit{i.h.}}}|\pi^{\prime}|+1=|\pi|
:

\sigma\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m^{\prime},e_{u}+e^{\prime})}S^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash^{(m_{r},e_{r})}r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}(y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m^{\prime}+m_{r},e_{u}+e^{\prime}+e_{r})}S^{\prime}\langle u\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

•

Contextual closure.
As in the
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{m}}
case, the only change is that the
m
index does not change, while the size of the derivation decreases. Note that indeed those cases do not depend on the details of the step itself, but only on the context enclosing it.

∎

\gettoappendix

prop:sillyopen-nfs-exists

Proof.

First of all, we recall the mutually inductive definition of weak answers and inert terms:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\hspace{.5cm}}r@{\hspace{.2cm}}l@{\hspace{.3cm}}lll}\textsc{Weak answers}\hskip 14.22636pt&a,a\hskip 5.69046pt&::=\hfil\hskip 8.5359pt&v&\lx@intercol\mid a[x{\shortleftarrow}i]\mbox{ with }x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(a)\hfil\lx@intercol\\
\hskip 14.22636pt&\hskip 5.69046pt&\hfil\hskip 8.5359pt&&\lx@intercol\mid a[x{\shortleftarrow}a^{\prime}]\mbox{ with }x\in\mathtt{fv}(a)\setminus\mathtt{shfv}(a)\hfil\lx@intercol\\[4.0pt]
\textsc{Inert terms}\hskip 14.22636pt&i,i^{\prime}\hskip 5.69046pt&::=\hfil\hskip 8.5359pt&x&\mid in&\mid i[x{\shortleftarrow}i^{\prime}]\mbox{ with }x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(i)\\
\hskip 14.22636pt&\hskip 5.69046pt&\hfil\hskip 8.5359pt&&&\mid i[x{\shortleftarrow}a]\mbox{ with }x\in\mathtt{fv}(i)\setminus\mathtt{shfv}(i)\\[0.0pt]
\textsc{Weak normal terms}\hskip 14.22636pt&n,n^{\prime}\hskip 5.69046pt&::=\hfil\hskip 8.5359pt&a&\mid i\end{array}

The proof is by mutual induction on
a
and
i
.

1.

Weak answers
. Cases:

•

Abstraction
, i.e.
a=\lambda x.t
. Then
\pi
is simply given by:

\mathsf{ax}_{\lambda}

{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{\lambda x.t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

•

Substitution of inert terms on answers
, i.e.
a=a^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}i]
with
x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime})
. By
i.h.
on
a^{\prime}
(Point 1), there is a derivation
\pi_{a^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
with
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}})=\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime})
. Therefore,
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}})
. By
i.h.
on
i
(Point 2) with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}:={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
, there is derivation
\pi_{i}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
with
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})=\mathtt{shfv}(i)
. Then
\pi
is given by:

\pi_{a^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

\pi_{a^{\prime\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{n}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}n]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

Note that
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})=\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}})\cup\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})=_{{\textit{i.h.}}}\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime})\cup\mathtt{shfv}(i)=\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}i])
(because
x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime})
by hypothesis).

•

Substitution of answers on answers
, i.e.
a=a^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}a^{\prime\prime}]
with
x\in\mathtt{fv}(a^{\prime})\setminus\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime})
. By
i.h.
on
a^{\prime}
(Point 1), there is a derivation
\pi_{a^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
with
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}})=\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime})
. Therefore,
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}})
. By
i.h.
on
a^{\prime\prime}
(Point 1), there is derivation
\pi_{a^{\prime\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
with
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}})=\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime\prime})
. Then
\pi
is given by:

\pi_{a^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

\pi_{a^{\prime\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{n}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}a^{\prime\prime}]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

Note that
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}})=\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}})\cup\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}})=_{{\textit{i.h.}}}\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime})\cup\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime\prime})=\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}a^{\prime\prime}])
(because
x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(a^{\prime})
by hypothesis).

2.

Inert terms
. Cases:

•

Variable
, i.e.
i=x
. Then, for any linear type
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
,
\pi
is simply given by:

\mathsf{ax}

{x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{x}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

•

Application
, i.e.
i=i^{\prime}n
. By
i.h.
on
n
(either Point 1 or Point 2 with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}:={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
), there is a derivation
\pi_{n}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{n}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{n}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
with
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{n})=\mathtt{shfv}(n)
. By
i.h.
(Point 2) on
i^{\prime}
with respect to the linear type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
, there is a derivation
\pi_{i^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
with
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}})=\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime})
. Then
\pi
is given by:

\pi_{i^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

\pi_{n}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{n}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{n}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{n}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i^{\prime}n}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

Note that
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{n})=\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}})\cup\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{n})=_{{\textit{i.h.}}}\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime})\cup\mathtt{shfv}(n)=\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime}n)
.

•

Substitution of inert terms on inert terms
, i.e.
i=i^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}i^{\prime\prime}]
with
x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime})
. By
i.h.
(Point 1) with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
, there is a derivation
\pi_{i^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
with
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}})=\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime})
. Therefore,
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}})
. By
i.h.
on
i^{\prime\prime}
(Point 2) with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}:={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
, there is derivation
\pi_{i^{\prime\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i^{\prime\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
with
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime\prime}})=\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime\prime})
. Then
\pi
is given by:

\pi_{i^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

\pi_{i^{\prime\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{n}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i^{\prime\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}i^{\prime\prime}]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

Note that
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime\prime}})=\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}})\cup\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime\prime}})=_{{\textit{i.h.}}}\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime})\cup\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime\prime})=\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}i^{\prime\prime}])
(because
x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime})
by hypothesis).

•

Substitution of answers on inert terms
, i.e.
i=i^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}a]
with
x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime})
. By
i.h.
(Point 1) with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
, there is a derivation
\pi_{i^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
with
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}})=\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime})
. Therefore,
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}})
. By
i.h.
on
a
(Point 1), there is derivation
\pi_{a}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
with
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a})=\mathtt{shfv}(a)
. Then
\pi
is given by:

\pi_{i^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

\pi_{a}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{i^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}a]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

Note that
\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a})=\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i^{\prime}})\cup\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a})=_{{\textit{i.h.}}}\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime})\cup\mathtt{shfv}(a)=\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}a])
(because
x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(i^{\prime})
by hypothesis).
∎

Lemma 40

(Merging multi-sets with respect to derivations)
.

Let
t
be a term, and any two derivations:

•

\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}
, and

•

\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}

Then there exists a derivation
\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
.

Proof.

The last rules of
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}
and
\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
can only be
\mathsf{many}
, thus it is enough to re-group their hypotheses.∎

Lemma 41

(linear anti-substitution)
.

If

\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}};x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}}
,
then there exists two derivations
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{t}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}
, and
\pi^{\prime}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{W};x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{W}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{t}
.

Proof.

By double induction on general (
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}
) types and weak contexts
W
.
Two cases, depending on whether
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}
is a linear or a multi type.

1.

Linear Type
, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
. Cases of
W
:

•

W=\langle\cdot\rangle
, then

\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}};x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
.
We have that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
by Lemma
9
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}=[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}]
.
We can construct the two derivations:
\pi^{\prime}
and
\rho

\mathsf{ax}

{x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}\vdash x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\mathsf{many}

{x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}\vdash x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}

and

\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}};x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

\mathsf{many}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}};x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}

with conditions satisfied on typing contexts.

•

W=W^{\prime}s
, then the typing derivation for
W^{\prime}\langle t\rangle s
is of the shape:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle t\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle t\rangle s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

By
i.h.
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{2}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{2}
.

Hence we can conclude easily by constructing the following derivation, as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{2}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle x\rangle s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

•

W=sW^{\prime}
, then the typing derivation for
sW^{\prime}\langle t\rangle
is of the shape:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle t\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash sW^{\prime}\langle t\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

By
i.h.
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{2},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{2}
.

Hence we can conclude easily by constructing the following derivation, as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{2}
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{2},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{2},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\vdash sW^{\prime}\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

•

W=W^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]
, then the typing derivation for
W^{\prime}\langle t\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}s]
is of the shape:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle t\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}(y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle t\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

By
i.h.
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{2}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{2}
. By Lemma
9
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{2}(y)={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
.

Hence we can conclude easily by constructing the following derivation, as
({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{2}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}(y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y),x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle x\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

•

W=s[y{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}]
, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}(y)={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{i\in I}
, then the typing derivation for
s[y{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}\langle t\rangle]
is of the shape:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle t\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}(y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\vdash s[y{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}\langle t\rangle]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

By
i.h.
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{2},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}(y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{2}
.

Hence we can conclude easily by constructing the following derivation, as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{2}
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{2},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}(y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{2},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\vdash s[y{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}\langle x\rangle]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

2.

Multi type
, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}
: let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{i\in I}
.

Then the derivation
\pi
must have the following shape:

({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i};x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{i}\vdash W\langle t\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i})_{i\in I}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{norm};x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{norm}\vdash W\langle t\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}};x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\vdash W\langle t\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}=(\uplus_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{norm}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}=(\uplus_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{norm}
.

By Point 1,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{norm},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{norm}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{norm}\vdash W\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{i},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{i}\vdash W\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}
for
{i\in I}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\prime}_{norm}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{norm}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\prime}_{i}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}
for
{i\in I}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{norm}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{norm}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\prime}_{norm}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{i}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\prime}_{i}
for
{i\in I}
.

By Lemma
40
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{t}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{t}:=(\uplus_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\prime}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\prime}_{norm}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}:=(\uplus_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{norm}
.

Then we can construct the following derivation for
W\langle x\rangle
:

({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{i},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{i}\vdash W\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i})_{i\in I}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{norm},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{norm}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}_{norm}\vdash W\langle x\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W};x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\vdash W\langle t\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}:=(\uplus_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{norm}
, hence
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{t}
.
∎

\gettoappendix

prop:open-subject-expansion

Proof.

We prove the following three points:

1.

Multiplicative
: if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{m}}s
then
there exists a derivation

\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
.

2.

Exponential
:
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{e}}s
then
there exists a derivation

\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
.

3.

Garbage collection by value
:
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s
then
there exists a derivation

\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
.

1.

Multiplicative
. Sub-points:

(a)

Root step
, that is,
t=S\langle\lambda x.u\rangle r\mapsto_{\mathtt{m}}S\langle u[x{\shortleftarrow}r]\rangle=s
. By induction on
S
. Two sub-cases:

•

Empty substitution context
, i.e.
S=\langle\cdot\rangle
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}(x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u[x{\shortleftarrow}r]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}
. We construct the
following derivation
\rho
, verifying the statement:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

\lambda

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{\lambda x.u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}(x)\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}(x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

@

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{(\lambda x.u)r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

•

Non-empty substitution context
, i.e.
S=S^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}p]
. Then
\pi
has the following structure:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{S^{\prime}\langle u[x{\shortleftarrow}r]\rangle}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{p}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\uplus}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{S^{\prime}\langle u[x{\shortleftarrow}r]\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}p]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}
.

By
i.h.
, we obtain a derivation

\sigma\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{S^{\prime}\langle\lambda x.u\rangle r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

Now, we go up the
\sigma
derivation, obtained by removing which must start by the rule (
@
):

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{S^{\prime}\langle\lambda x.u\rangle}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{S^{\prime}\langle\lambda x.u\rangle r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{1}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}
.

Now, we apply a rule
\mathsf{ES}
with respect to
y
and
p
, obtaining the following derivation
\rho
, satisfying the statement:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{S^{\prime}\langle\lambda x.u\rangle}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{p}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}(y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{S^{\prime}\langle\lambda x.u\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}p]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{S^{\prime}\langle\lambda x.u\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}p]r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

(b)

Contextual closure
. We have
t=W\langle u\rangle\rightarrow_{\mathtt{m}}W\langle r\rangle=s
. Cases of
W
:

•

Empty
, that is,
t\mapsto_{\mathtt{m}}s
. Then it follows from the previous point.

•

Left of an application
, that is,
W=W^{\prime}p
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{p}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}
.

By
i.h.
, there exists a derivation
\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
, thus allowing us to construct
\rho
as follows:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{p}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

•

Right of an application
, that is,
W=pW^{\prime}
. By setting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{{i\in I}}
and
I=\{1,..,k\}
,
\pi
has the following form:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i})_{{i\in I}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

{(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\vdash pW^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
. By
i.h.
there are derivations of final judgements

for
{i\in I}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
, so that we obtain the following derivation:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i})_{{i\in I}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

{(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\vdash pW^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

Left of a substitution

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}p]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
and

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}p]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

and

\mathsf{ES}

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\vdash p[x{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}\langle r\rangle]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
