{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i})_{{i\in I}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

{(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\vdash W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}(x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus x)\uplus(\uplus_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i})\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\vdash p[x{\shortleftarrow}W^{\prime}\langle u\rangle]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

2.

Exponential.
Cases:

•

Root step
, i.e.
t=W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\mapsto_{\mathtt{e}_{W}}W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\vdash W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
.

By the linear anti-substitution lemma (
41
) applied to
{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
, there exist two derivations:

(a)

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}
and

(b)

\pi_{W}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{W};x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
.

such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{W}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{W}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{u}

From derivations
\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}
, we can build a derivation
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}
.

Then
\rho
is built as follows:

\pi_{W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{W};x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{u}}\vdash u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash W\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

Where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{W}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{u}
.

•

Contextual closure.
As in the
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{m}}
case. Note that indeed those cases do not depend on the details of the step itself, but only on the context enclosing it.

3.

Garbage collection by value
. Cases:

•

Root step
, i.e.
t=u[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle u\rangle=s
. By induction on
S
. Cases:

(a)

S=\langle\cdot\rangle
. Then
t=u[x{\shortleftarrow}v]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}u=s
with
x\notin\mathtt{fv}(u)
.

Then the final judgment of the derivation
\pi
is
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
.

Then, the derivation
\rho
for
t
has the following shape:

\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\mathsf{ax}_{\lambda}

{}\vdash v\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

{}\vdash v\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash u[x{\shortleftarrow}v]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

(b)

S=S^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}r]
. Then
t=u[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S^{\prime}\langle u\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]=s
with
x\notin\mathtt{fv}(u)
. The derivation
\pi
for
s
has the following shape:

\sigma\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash S^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}(y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash S^{\prime}\langle u\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}
.

By
i.h.
, there exists a derivation of
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash u[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
. By Lemma
9
, given a derivation
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
one has
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}(x)={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
. Then, it is of the following shape:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}}\vdash S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}}\vdash S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}}\vdash u[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}
.

Hence we can construct the following derivation
\rho
for
t
:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}}\vdash S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}(y)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}}\vdash u[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle[y{\shortleftarrow}r]]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}_{r}
.

•

Contextual closure.
As in the
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{m}}
case. Note that indeed those cases do not depend on the details of the step itself, but only on the context enclosing it.

∎

Appendix D
Proofs of Sect.
7
(Call-by-Value and Operational Equivalence)

Lemma 42

(Substitution for CbV)
.

Let
\pi_{t}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}};x:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}}
and
\pi_{v}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m^{\prime}}{v}\ {:}\ {{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}}
. Then there exists a derivation

\pi_{t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m^{\prime\prime}}{t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}}
with

m^{\prime\prime}~\leq~m+m^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
\pi_{t}
. We first distinguish between linear and multi types.

1.

Linear Type
, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
. Let us
write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}
as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{{i\in I}}
for some non-empty set of indices
I
. We reason by cases of the
last rule of
\pi_{t}
:

•

Rule
\mathtt{ax}
. Two cases:

–

t=x
. Then
t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}=x\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}=v
and
\pi_{t}\,\triangleright{{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{0}{x}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}}
. Thus,
|I|=1
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}
, and the
hypothesis
\pi_{v}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m}{v}\ {:}\ {{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}}
is obtained by applying a unary
\mathtt{many}
rule to a derivation

\rho_{v}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m}{v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
. Take the typing derivation
\pi_{t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}:=\rho_{v}
. Note that
m^{\prime\prime}=m=0+m=m^{\prime}+m
.

–

t=y
. Then
t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}=y\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}=y
and
\pi_{t}\,\triangleright{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}^{\prime},y:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}};x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0}{y}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}}
. Take the typing derivation

\pi_{t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}:={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}^{\prime},y:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m^{\prime\prime}}{y}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
.

Note that
m^{\prime\prime}=0\leq m+m^{\prime}
.

•

Rule
\lambda
. Then
t=\lambda y.u
, and
\pi_{t}
has the following form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y;x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m}{\lambda y.u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}(y)\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}};x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}\end{array}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}(y)\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}
. By
i.h.
there exists
\pi_{u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\,\triangleright{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{m^{\prime\prime}}u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}
such that

m^{\prime\prime}\leq m+m^{\prime}

from which by applying
\lambda
we obtain:

\begin{array}[]{c}\pi_{t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\,\triangleright{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\setminus\!\!\!\setminus y}\vdash^{m}\lambda y.u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}(y)\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}\end{array}

The derivation
\pi_{t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}
satisfies the equation in the statement because of the
i.h.
and the fact that

m^{\prime\prime}\leq m+m^{\prime}
.

•

Rule
@
. Then
t=ur
. The
left premise of the
@
rule in
\pi_{t}
assigns a type
u:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
:

\small{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r};x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m}{ur}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}\lx@proof@logical@and\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u};x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m_{u}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}\pi_{r}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r};x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m_{r}}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

First, we apply multi-set splitting (Lemma
38
) to
\pi_{v}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m^{\prime}}{v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}
, obtaining two derivations
\pi_{v}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m_{1}^{\prime}}{v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{u}}
and
\pi_{v}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m_{2}^{\prime}}{v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{r}}
and such that
m^{\prime}=m_{1}^{\prime}+m_{2}^{\prime}
.

By
i.h.
, we can substitute these derivations
\pi_{u}
and
\pi_{r}
into the premises of
the
@
rule, obtaining derivations
\pi_{u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}
and
\pi_{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}
.

By applying
@
, we obtain:

\pi_{u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m_{u}^{\prime}}{u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

\pi_{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m_{r}^{\prime}}{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

@

\pi_{t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m^{\prime\prime}}{u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}=(ur)\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

Which satisfies the statement, as by
i.h.

m_{u}^{\prime}\leq m_{u}+m_{1}^{\prime}
and
m_{r}\leq m_{r}+m_{2}^{\prime}
the following calculation shows:

\begin{array}[]{llll}m^{\prime\prime}&=&m_{u}^{\prime}+m_{r}^{\prime}+1\\
&\leq&m_{u}+m_{1}^{\prime}+m_{r}+m_{2}^{\prime}+1\\
&=&m+m^{\prime}\end{array}

2.

Multi type
, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}
: Then
\pi_{t}
must end with the rule (
\mathsf{many}
) with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime\prime}}}_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{j=1,\ldots,k}
. The multi-set
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}
assigned to

x
can be partioned in
k+2
(potentially empty) multi-sets

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{1},\dots{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{k}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{norm}
to be distributed among the
premises of the
\mathsf{many}
rule of
\pi
as follows:

\pi_{r}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r};x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}\lx@proof@logical@and(\pi_{r}^{j}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}^{j};x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{j}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m_{j}}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime\prime}}}_{j}})_{j=1,\dots,k}\pi_{r}^{norm}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}^{norm};x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{norm}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m_{norm}}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

where the notations satisfy
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}=\uplus_{j=1}^{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}^{j}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}^{norm}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}=\uplus_{j=1}^{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{j}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{norm}
.

Moreover, given the partition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}
into

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{k},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{norm}
, the other derivation

\pi_{v}\,\triangleright{}\vdash^{m^{\prime}}v\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}
from the statement hypotheses gives rise, by the multi-set splitting lemma (Lemma
38
),
to derivations
\rho_{u}^{norm}\,\triangleright{}\vdash^{m^{\prime}_{norm}}v{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{norm}
and
({\rho_{r}}^{j}\,\triangleright{}\vdash^{m^{\prime}_{j}}v{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{j})_{j=1,\ldots,k}
with
m^{\prime}=m^{\prime}_{norm}+\sum_{j=1,\ldots,k}m^{\prime}_{j}
.

By
i.h.
, we can substitute these derivations
\rho_{r}^{norm}
and
{\rho_{r}}^{j}
into the premises of
the
\mathsf{many}
rule, obtaining derivations
\pi_{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}^{norm}
and
\pi_{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}^{j}
:

\pi_{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m^{\prime\prime}}{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}\lx@proof@logical@and(\pi_{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}^{j}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}^{j}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m^{\prime\prime}_{j}}{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime\prime}}}_{j}})_{j=1,\ldots,k}\pi_{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}^{norm}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}^{norm}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m^{\prime\prime}_{norm}}{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

and, by the previous application of
i.h.
we have that
m^{\prime\prime}_{norm}\leq m_{norm}+m^{\prime}_{norm}
and for all
j

m^{\prime\prime}_{j}\leq m_{j}+m^{\prime}_{j}
.
Hence we can conclude as
m^{\prime\prime}=m^{\prime\prime}_{norm}+\sum_{j=1,\ldots,k}m^{\prime\prime}_{j}\leq m_{norm}+\sum_{j=1,\ldots,k}m_{j}+\sum_{j=1,\ldots,k}m^{\prime}_{j}+m^{\prime}_{norm}=m+m^{\prime}
.

∎

\gettoappendix

prop:value-subject-reduction

Proof.

By induction on
t\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}s
.
Cases:

•

Rule at top level
:

t=(\lambda x.u)v\mapsto_{\beta_{v}}u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}=s

By hypothesis,
\pi\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{(\lambda x.u)v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
and
\pi
must end with rule
@
:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\vdash^{m_{u}}u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\lambda

\vdash^{m_{u}}\lambda x.u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{v}\,\triangleright\vdash^{m_{v}}v\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

\vdash^{m}(\lambda x.u)v\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

Applying the substitution lemma (Lemma
42
) to
\pi_{u}
and
\pi_{v}
, we obtain

\pi_{u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\,\triangleright\vdash^{m^{\prime}}u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

such that

m^{\prime}\leq_{{L.\ref{l:value-substitution}}}m_{u}+m_{r}=m-1
. The statement holds with
\pi^{\prime}:=\pi_{u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}
.

•

Reduction in Left:

u\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}u^{\prime}

(@l)

t=ur\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}u^{\prime}r=s

By hypothesis,
\pi\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{ur}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
and
\pi
must end with rule
@
:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright\vdash u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{r}\,\triangleright\vdash r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

\vdash ur\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

The statement then follows from the
i.h.
applied to
\pi_{u}
.

•

Reduction in Right:

r\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}r^{\prime}

(@r)

t=ur\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}ur^{\prime}=s

By hypothesis,
\pi\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{ur}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
and
\pi
must end with rule
@
:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright\vdash u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

(\pi_{r}^{i}\,\triangleright\vdash r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}_{i})_{i\in I}

\pi_{r}^{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}\,\triangleright\vdash r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

\vdash r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{{i\in I}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

\vdash ur\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

The statement then follows from the
i.h.
applied to the derivations
\pi_{r}^{i}
and
\pi_{r}^{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
.

∎

\gettoappendix

thm:value-correctness

Proof.

By induction on
m
. If

t
is a
\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}
normal form—that covers the base case
m=1
, for which
t
is
necessarily an abstraction and
\pi
simply is a
\mathsf{ax}_{\lambda}
rule—then, by taking
s:=t
and
the empty reduction sequence, the statement holds (in particular
|d|=0=m
).

Otherwise,
t\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}u
and by quantitative subject reduction
(Prop.
16
) there is a derivation
\pi^{\prime}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{m^{\prime}}{u}\ {:}\ {A}

such that

m^{\prime}<m
. By

i.h.
, there exists an abstraction
v
and a reduction sequence
e:u\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}^{*}v
such that
|e|\leq m^{\prime}
. Just note that

t\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}u\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}^{|e|}v
, that is, there is a reduction sequence
d:t\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}^{*}v
such that
|d|=|e|+1\leq_{\textit{i.h.}}m^{\prime}+1\leq m
. ∎

Lemma 43

(Anti-Substitution for CbV)
.

Let
v
be a closed value and
t
a term. If
\pi_{1}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}}
then there exists two derivations
\pi_{t}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}};x:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}}
and
\pi_{v}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{v}\ {:}\ {{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}}
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the term
t
and the type
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}
. Cases of
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}
:

1.

Linear Type
, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
. Cases of
t
:

•

Variable
. Two subcases:

–

t=x
, then set
\pi_{v}:=\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{t}:=\begin{array}[]{c}{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{x}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}\end{array}
.

–

t=y
, then
\pi_{t}:=\pi_{1}={y:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{y}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
and
\pi_{v}:=\vdash v\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
.

•

Abstraction
, that is
t=\lambda y.u
, there are two possibilities for the last rule of the derivation
\pi_{1}
:

–

\begin{array}[]{c}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{\lambda y.u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}\pi_{2}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}};y:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}\end{array}

By induction, from
\pi_{2}
we get two derivations
\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}};y:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}};x:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
and
\pi_{v}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{v}\ {:}\ {{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}}
and we can conclude easily by adding a
\lambda
rule to the derivation
\pi_{u}
.

–

\begin{array}[]{c}{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{\lambda y.u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}\end{array}

Then
\pi_{t}:={}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{\lambda y.u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
and
\pi_{v}:={}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}{}
as
v
is a closed value (hence an abstraction).

•

Application
, that is
t=ur

two cases for the last rule of
\pi_{1}
:

–

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{(ur)\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}\lx@proof@logical@and\pi_{2}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}\pi_{3}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{r\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

By
i.h.
, there exists
\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{1}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
and
\pi_{r}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{2}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}
and
\pi_{v1}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{1}}
and
\pi_{v2}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{2}}
.

By Lemma
40
(merging multi types), we have that
{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{1}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{2}
.

Now, we can conclude by defining
\pi_{t}
as the following:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}\lx@proof@logical@and\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{1}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}\pi_{r}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{2}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

2.

Multi Type
, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}
. Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{i\in I}
Then the derivation
\pi_{1}
starts with the
\mathsf{many}
rule.

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}(\pi_{i}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}}){i\in I}

By
i.h.
, there exists derivations
\rho_{i}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}}
and
\pi_{vi}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}}
for all
{i\in I}
.

Then we can build the derivation
\pi_{t}
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}:=\uplus_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}
.

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}(\pi_{i}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{i},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}_{i}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}_{i}}){i\in I}

By Lemma
40
(merging multi types), we can build a derivation
\pi_{v}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{N}}}
, concluding the proof. ∎

\gettoappendix

prop:value-subject-expansion

Proof.

By induction on
t\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}s
.
Cases:

•

Rule at top level
:

t=(\lambda x.u)v\mapsto_{\beta_{v}}u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}=s

By hypothesis,

\pi\,\triangleright\vdash u\{x{\shortleftarrow}v\}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
.
Applying the anti-substitution lemma (Lemma
43
) to
\pi
, we obtain
\pi_{u}
and
\pi_{v}
.
We can then build the following
\rho\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{(\lambda x.u)v}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
derivation:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\vdash u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\lambda

\vdash\lambda x.u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{v}\,\triangleright\vdash v\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

\vdash(\lambda x.u)v\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

•

Reduction in Left:

u\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}u^{\prime}

(@l)

t=ur\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}u^{\prime}r=s

By hypothesis,
\pi\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{{u^{\prime}}r}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
and
\pi
must end with rule
@
:

\pi_{{u^{\prime}}}\,\triangleright\vdash{u^{\prime}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{r}\,\triangleright\vdash r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

\vdash{u^{\prime}}r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

The statement then follows from the
i.h.
applied to
\pi_{u^{\prime}}
.

•

Reduction in Right:

r\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}r^{\prime}

(@r)

t=ur\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}ur^{\prime}=s

By hypothesis,
\pi\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
and
\pi
must end with rule
@
:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright\vdash u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{{i\in I}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

(\pi_{{r^{\prime}}}^{i}\,\triangleright\vdash{r^{\prime}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}_{i})_{i\in I}

\pi_{{r^{\prime}}}^{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}\,\triangleright\vdash{r^{\prime}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

\vdash{r^{\prime}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}_{{i\in I}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

\vdash u{r^{\prime}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

The statement then follows from the
i.h.
applied to the derivations
\pi_{r^{\prime}}^{i}
and
\pi_{{r^{\prime}}}^{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
.

∎

\gettoappendix

thm:value-completeness

Proof.

If
t
is SN for
\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}
then there is a reduction
d\colon t\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}^{*}n
with
n

\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}
-normal.
By induction on
k=|d|
.

•

k=0
:

t
is a closed
\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}
-normal form, hence
t
is
necessarily an abstraction and by taking
\pi
as simply a
\mathsf{ax}_{\lambda}
rule—then, the statement holds.

•

k>0
:
t\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}u\rightarrow_{\beta_{v}}^{k-1}v
and by
i.h.
there exists a derivation
\pi^{\prime}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
.
By subject expansion
(Prop.
18
) there is a derivation
\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
.∎

Theorem 44
.

Conversion is included in the silly contextual equivalence:

t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}t^{\prime}
implies
t\simeq_{C}^{\mathtt{silly}}s

Proof.

Suppose
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}t^{\prime}
, we need to show that for all
C
,
C\langle t\rangle
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normalizing iff
C\langle t^{\prime}\rangle
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normalizing (i.e.
t\simeq_{C}^{\mathtt{silly}}t^{\prime}
).

It is sufficient to prove that for all
C
, for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}},

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash C\langle t\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash C\langle t^{\prime}\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
. Indeed, it then implies by correctness and completeness of the silly multi types for the weak case (Th.
11
and Th.
14
) that
C\langle t\rangle
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normalizing iff
C\langle t^{\prime}\rangle
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normalizing.

We prove the intermediary proposition (for all
C
, for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}},

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash C\langle t\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash C\langle t^{\prime}\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
) by a straightforward induction on contexts:

•

C=\langle\cdot\rangle
: by subject reduction and expansion of the silly multi types for the weak case (Prop.
10
and Prop.
13
) we have that for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}},

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash t\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\vdash t^{\prime}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
.

•

C=\lambda x.C^{\prime}
: all derivations of
C\langle t\rangle
and
C\langle t^{\prime}\rangle
must start with the typing rule
\mathsf{ax}_{\lambda}
or the typing rule
\lambda
. For
\mathsf{ax}_{\lambda}
, it is trivial to conclude. For
\lambda
, one can apply the induction hypothesis on the premise of the rule and conclude.

•

C=C^{\prime}s
: all derivations of
C\langle t\rangle
and
C\langle t^{\prime}\rangle
must start with the typing rule
@
. One can apply the induction hypothesis on the left premise of the rule and the right premise types
s
in both derivations, hence we can conclude.

•

Other cases follow the same argument.∎

Appendix E
Proofs of Sect.
8
(The Call-by-Silly Strategy)

E.1
Characterization of Normal Forms

Let us recall the definition of strict answer (in the paper is given in the statement of Prop.
23
which appears after the next lemma, where the notion is needed):

\begin{array}[]{r@{\hspace{.5cm}} rll}\textsc{Strict answers}\hskip 14.22636pt&a_{s}&::=&v\mid a_{s}[x{\shortleftarrow}a_{s}^{\prime}]\ \mbox{ with }x\in\mathtt{fv}(a_{s})\end{array}

Lemma 45
.

1.

If
t
is an answer then
t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
-normal.

2.

If
t
is a strict answer then
t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normal.

Proof.

1.

By induction on
t
. Cases:

•

Value
, that is,
t=\lambda x.s
. Then
t
is a
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
-normal.

•

Substitution
, that is,
t=a[x{\shortleftarrow}a^{\prime}]
. By
i.h.
,
a
and
a^{\prime}
are
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
-normal. By Lemma
32
,
x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(a)
so that there are not
\mapsto_{\mathtt{e}_{W}}
redex involving
[x{\shortleftarrow}a^{\prime}]
in
t
. Then
t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
-normal.

2.

By induction on
t
. Cases:

•

Value
, that is,
t=\lambda x.s
. Then
t
is a
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normal.

•

Substitution
, that is,
t=a_{s}[x{\shortleftarrow}a_{s}^{\prime}]
with
x\in\mathtt{fv}(a_{s})
. By
i.h.
,
a_{s}
and
a_{s}^{\prime}
are
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normal. By Lemma
32
,
x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(a_{s})
so that there are not
\mapsto_{\mathtt{e}_{W}}
redex involving
[x{\shortleftarrow}a_{s}^{\prime}]
in
t
. By hypothesis,
x\in\mathtt{fv}(a_{s})
so that neither there is a
\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
redex involving
[x{\shortleftarrow}a_{s}^{\prime}]
in
t
. Then
t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normal.∎

Lemma 46
.

Let
t
be closed. If
t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{m},\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
-normal then it has shape
S\langle\lambda x.s\rangle
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
. Cases:

•

Variable
, that is,
t=x
. Impossible because
t
is closed.

•

Abstraction
, that is,
t=\lambda x.s
. Then
t
has the required shape.

•

Application
, that is,
t=su
. Since
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{m},\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
can evaluate
s
in
t
,
s
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{m},\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
-normal. By
i.h.
,
s
has shape
S\langle\lambda x.r\rangle
and then
t
has a
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{m}}}
-redex—absurd. Then this case is impossible.

•

Substitution
, that is,
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Since
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{m},\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
can evaluate
s
in
t
,
s
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{m},\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
-normal. By
i.h.
,
s
has shape
S\langle\lambda x.r\rangle
, and so does
t
.∎

\gettoappendix

prop:closed-nfs

Proof.

1.

We prove the following implications:
t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
-normal
\Rightarrow

t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}
-normal
\Rightarrow

t
is an answer
\Rightarrow

t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
-normal. Sub-points:

(a)

\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
-normal
\Rightarrow

\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}
-normal
. Trivial, because
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}
is a sub-relation of
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
.

(b)

\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}
-normal
\Rightarrow
answer
. By induction on
t
. Cases:

•

Variable
, that is,
t=x
. Impossible because
t
is closed.

•

Abstraction
, that is,
t=\lambda x.s
. Then
t
is an answer.

•

Application
, that is,
t=su
. Since
s
is normal for
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{m},\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
,
s
has shape
S\langle\lambda x.r\rangle
by Lemma
46
, and then
t
has a
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{m}}}
-redex—absurd. Then this case is impossible.

•

Substitution
, that is,
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Since
\langle\cdot\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}u]
is a silly evaluation context,
s
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}
-normal. By
i.h.
,
s
is an answer. Then
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}
does evaluate
u
, which implies that
u
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}
-normal. By
i.h.
,
u
is an answer. Then
t
is an answer.

(c)

Answer
\Rightarrow

\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
-normal
. By Lemma
45
.
1
.

2.

We prove the following implications:
t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normal
\Rightarrow

t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}}
-normal
\Rightarrow

t
is strict a answer
\Rightarrow

t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normal. Sub-points:

(a)

\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normal
\Rightarrow

\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}}
-normal
. Trivial, because
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}}
is a sub-relation of
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
.

(b)

\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}}
-normal
\Rightarrow
strict answer
. By induction on
t
. Cases:

•

Variable
, that is,
t=x
. Impossible because
t
is closed.

•

Abstraction
, that is,
t=\lambda x.s
. Then
t
is a strict answer.

•

Application
, that is,
t=su
. This case goes as for the application sub-case of the previous point.

•

Substitution
, that is,
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Since
\langle\cdot\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}u]
is a silly evaluation context,
s
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}}
-normal. By
i.h.
,
s
is a strict answer, thus an answer. Then
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}}
does evaluate

, which implies that

is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}}
-normal. By

,

is a strict answer. In particular,

has shape

for some

and

. Now, if
x\notin\mathtt{fv}(s)
then there is a
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}
redex, which would be against hypotheses. Then
x\in\mathtt{fv}(s)
. Then
t
is a strict answer.

(c)

Strict answer
\Rightarrow

\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}}
-normal
. By Lemma

.

.∎

E.2
Non-Erasing Determinism and Erasing Diamond

Lemma 47
.

1.

Let
t=A\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
and
t=A^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
. Then
A=A^{\prime}
and
x=y
.

2.

Let
t=N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
and
t=N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
. Then
N=N^{\prime}
and
x=y
.

3.

Let
t=Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
and
t=Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
. Then
Y=Y^{\prime}
and
x=y
.

Proof.

1.

By induction on
t
. The only non immediate case is
t=s[z{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Cases of
A
:

(a)

A=A_{1}[z{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Then
x\in\mathtt{shfv}(s)
. By Lemma
32
,
s
is not an answer. Then necessarily
A^{\prime}=A^{\prime}_{1}[z{\shortleftarrow}u]
and the statement follows from the
i.h.

(b)

A=s[z{\shortleftarrow}A_{1}]
and
s
is an answer. By Lemma
32
,
y\notin\mathtt{shfv}(s)
. Then necessarily
A^{\prime}=s[z{\shortleftarrow}A^{\prime}_{1}]
and the statement follows from the
i.h.

2.

By induction on
t
. Cases:

•

Variable
, that is,
t=x
. Evident

•

Abstraction
, that is,
t=\lambda x.s
. The hypotheses are not verified, so the statement trivially holds.

•

Application
, that is,
t=su
. Since name contexts do not enter into arguments, both
N
and
N^{\prime}
are obtained by applying a name context to
u
. Then the statement follows from the
i.h.

•

Substitution
, that is,
t=s[z{\shortleftarrow}u]
. As the previous point.

3.

By induction on
t
. Cases:

•

Variable
, that is,
t=x
. Evident

•

Abstraction
, that is,
t=\lambda x.s
. The hypotheses are not verified, so the statement trivially holds.

•

Application
, that is,
t=su
. Since silly contexts do not enter into arguments, both
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
are obtained by applying a name context to
u
. Then the statement follows from Point
2
.

•

Substitution
, that is,
t=s[z{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Cases of
Y
:

(a)

Y=Y_{1}[z{\shortleftarrow}u]
with
Y_{1}
either a
N
context or a
A
context. Then
x\in\mathtt{shfv}(s)
. By Lemma
32
,
s
is not an answer. Then necessarily
Y^{\prime}=Y^{\prime}_{1}[z{\shortleftarrow}u]
and the statement follows from the
i.h.

(b)

Y=s[z{\shortleftarrow}Y_{1}]
and
s
is an answer. By Lemma
32
,
y\notin\mathtt{shfv}(s)
. Then necessarily
Y^{\prime}=s[z{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime}_{1}]
and the statement follows from the
i.h.
∎

Lemma 48
.

1.

Let
t=N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
. Then
t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}
-normal.

2.

Let
t=Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
. Then
t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{AY}}}
-normal and
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
-normal.

Proof.

1.

By induction on
t
. Cases:

•

Variable
, that is,
t=x
. Evident

•

Abstraction
, that is,
t=\lambda x.s
. The hypotheses are not verified, so the statement trivially holds.

•

Application
, that is,
t=su
. Then
N=N^{\prime}u
. By
i.h.
,
N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}
-normal, and so is
t
.

•

Substitution
, that is,
t=s[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Then
N=N^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
. By
i.h.
,
N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}
-normal, and so is
t
. If there is a
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}
-step in
t
it can then only be a root step, for which
s=N^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
. Now, we also have
s=N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
by hypothesis, so that Lemma
47
.
2
gives
N^{\prime}=N^{\prime\prime}
and
x=y
, which is absurd because then
t\neq N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
since
x=y
is bound by
[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
in
t
, against hypotheses. Then
t
has no root
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}
-step, and so it is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}
-normal.

2.

By induction on
t
. Cases:

•

Variable
, that is,
t=x
. Evident

•

Abstraction
, that is,
t=\lambda x.s
. The hypotheses are not verified, so the statement trivially holds.

•

Application
, that is,
t=su
. Then
Y=Nu
and
t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{AY}}}
-normal. By Point
1
,
s
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}
-normal, thus
t
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
-normal.

•

Substitution
, that is,
t=s[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Cases of
Y
:

(a)

Y=Y^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
with
Y^{\prime}
either a
N
context or a
A
context. By
i.h.
,
s
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
-normal. Since
x\in\mathtt{shfv}(s)
, by Lemma
32

s
is not an answer. Therefore, if there is an exponential step in
t
it does not come from
u
. It can then only be a root step, for which either
s=N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
or
s=Y^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
; the second case actually subsumes the first one. Now, we also have
s=Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
by hypothesis, so that Lemma
47
.
3
gives
Y^{\prime}=Y^{\prime\prime}
and
x=y
, which is absurd because then
t\neq Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
since
x=y
is bound by
[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
in
t
, against hypotheses. Then
t
has no root exponential step, and so it is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{AY}}}
-normal and
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
-normal.

(b)

Y=s[y{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime}]
and
s
is an answer. By Lemma
45
.
1
,
s
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
-normal. By Lemma
32
,
y\notin\mathtt{shfv}(s)
and so there are no root exponential steps. By
i.h.
,
u
is
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{AY}}}
-normal and
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
-normal. Then so is
t
.∎

Lemma 49

(Answers are invariant by
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
)
.

Let
a
be an answer.

1.

If
a\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t
then
t
is an answer.

2.

If
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}a
then
t
is an answer.

Proof.

1.

By induction on
a
. Cases:

•

Value
, that is,
a=\lambda x.s
. Then
a
does not reduce and the statement trivially holds.

•

Substitution
, that is
a=a^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}a^{\prime\prime}]
. If
a\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t
because of a reduction in
a^{\prime}
or
a^{\prime\prime}
then the statement follows by the
i.h.
Otherwise, we have
x\notin a^{\prime}
,
a^{\prime\prime}=S\langle v\rangle
, and
a=a^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle a^{\prime}\rangle
, which is an answer because all the ESs in
S
contain answers.

2.

By induction on
t
. If
t
is a variable or an abstraction then it cannot reduce. If
t
is an application then
a
would be an application, which is absurd; so this case is impossible. Then
t
is a substitution
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}a
:

•

Root
. Then
x\notin\mathtt{fv}(s)
,
u=S\langle v\rangle
, and
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle s\rangle=a
. Then
s
is an answer and all the substitutions in
S
contain answers. Then
u
is an answer, and so is
t
.

•

Because
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}
. Then
a=s^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]
and
u
is an answer. By
i.h.
,
s
is an answer, and so is
t
.

•

Because
u\rightarrow_{\mathtt{w}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}u^{\prime}
. Then
a=s[x{\shortleftarrow}u^{\prime}]
and
s
is an answer. By
i.h.
,
u
is an answer, and so is
t
. ∎

To prove that
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}
is diamond, we first have to prove that
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
is diamond, where
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
is defined as follows.

Definition 50

(
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
)
.

Reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
is defined as the closure by CbN contexts
N
of
\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
, that is,
N\langle t\rangle\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}N\langle s\rangle
if
t\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s
.

Lemma 51
.

Reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
is diamond.

Proof.

We prove that if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}
and
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime\prime}
with
t^{\prime}\neq t^{\prime\prime}
then there exists
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
t^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}
. By induction on
t
. Cases:

•

Variable
, that is,
t=x
. Trivial, because
t
does not reduce.

•

Abstraction
, that is,
t=\lambda x.s
. Trivial, because
t
does not reduce.

•

Application
, that is,
t=su
. Then both steps come from
s
and the statement follows from the
i.h.

•

Substitution
, that is,
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}
:

–

Root
. Then
x\notin s
and
u=S\langle v\rangle
, so that
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle s\rangle
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime\prime}
:

*

Root
. Then the statement holds.

*

Because
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime\prime}
. Then the diamond diagram follows:
s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]
S\langle s\rangle
s^{\prime\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]
S\langle s^{\prime\prime}\rangle
\mathtt{y}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{y}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{y}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{y}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}

–

Because
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime\prime}
:

*

Root
. Already treated above.

*

Because
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime\prime}
. It follows from the
i.h.
∎

\gettoappendix

is not a silly evaluation context.

Root
. Then the two steps coincide.

E.3
Postponement of
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}

For proving the postponement of
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}
, we actually also have to consider the postponement of
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
, defined in Def.
50
.

We have to consider
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
because the definition of CbS contexts relies on CbN contexts. Precisely, when the postponement of silly steps happens in the left sub-term
s
of an application
su
, the involved steps are in CbN contexts for
s
and have to swap into CbN contexts. Unfortunately, this fact triggers the need for various auxiliary lemmas.

Lemma 52

(Backward deformation of CbN contexts)
.

If
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
then
t=N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
for some CbN context
N^{\prime}
such that
N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
for every
s
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
.
Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
:

•

Root
, that is,
t=u[y{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle u\rangle
with
y\notin\mathtt{fv}(u)
. Since
N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=S\langle u\rangle
, the hole of
N
falls in
u
, that is,
u=N^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
and
N=S\langle N^{\prime\prime}\rangle
. Then
N^{\prime}:=N^{\prime\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]
which is indeed a CbN context. Moreover,

N^{\prime}\langle s\rangle=N^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle N^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\rangle=N\langle s\rangle

•

Left of an application
, that is,
t=su\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}su=t^{\prime}
with
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}
. It follows from the
i.h.

•

Left of a substitution
, that is,
t=s[y{\shortleftarrow}u]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]=t^{\prime}
with
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}
.
It follows from the
i.h.
∎

Lemma 53

(Local postponement of name GC by value)
.

1.

Name multiplicative
: if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}t^{\prime\prime}
then
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime\prime}
for some
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

2.

Name exponential
: if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}t^{\prime\prime}
then
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime\prime}
for some
t^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

1.

Name multiplicative
. By induction on
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}
:

•

Root
, that is,
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle s\rangle
with
x\notin\mathtt{fv}(s)
. The only possibility for
S\langle s\rangle\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}t^{\prime\prime}
is that it takes place in
s
, that is,
S\langle s\rangle\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}S\langle s^{\prime}\rangle=t^{\prime\prime}
because
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}s^{\prime}
. Then:
s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]
S\langle s\rangle
s^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]
S\langle s^{\prime}\rangle
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}

•

Left of an application
, that is,
t=su\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}u=t^{\prime}
with
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}
. Cases of
s^{\prime}u\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}t^{\prime\prime}
:

(a)

Step in
s^{\prime}
. Then it follows from the
i.h.

(b)

Step at the root
. Then
s^{\prime}=S\langle\lambda y.r\rangle
for some
S^{\prime}
and
r
, and
t^{\prime}=S\langle\lambda y.r\rangle u\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}S\langle r[y{\shortleftarrow}u]\rangle=t^{\prime\prime}
. The only possibility for
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle\lambda y.r\rangle
is that
S=S^{\prime\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime\prime}\langle v\rangle]\rangle
and that the swap diagram is as follows:
S^{\prime\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime}\langle\lambda y.r\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime\prime}\langle v\rangle]\rangle u
S^{\prime\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime}\langle\lambda y.r\rangle\rangle\rangle u
S^{\prime\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime}\langle r[y{\shortleftarrow}u]\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime\prime}\langle v\rangle]\rangle
S^{\prime\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime}\langle r[y{\shortleftarrow}u]\rangle\rangle\rangle
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}

•

Left of a substitution
, that is,
t=s[y{\shortleftarrow}u]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]=t^{\prime}
. The step
s^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}t^{\prime\prime}
comes form a step in
s^{\prime}
. Then it follows from the
i.h.

2.

Name exponential
. Let
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}t^{\prime\prime}
. By induction on
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}t^{\prime}
:

•

Root
, that is,
t=s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle s\rangle
with
x\notin\mathtt{fv}(s)
. Cases of
S\langle s\rangle\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}t^{\prime\prime}
:

(a)

Step in
s
, that is,
S\langle s\rangle\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}S\langle s^{\prime}\rangle=t^{\prime\prime}
because
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}s^{\prime}
. Then:
s[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]
S\langle s\rangle
s^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]
S\langle s^{\prime}\rangle
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}

(b)

Step involving a substitution in
S
acting on
s
, that is,
S\langle s\rangle=S\langle C\langle x\rangle\rangle\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}S\langle C\langle u\rangle\rangle=t^{\prime\prime}
. Impossible because
S
cannot capture free variables of
s
.

•

Left of an application
, that is,
t=su\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}u=t^{\prime}
. Then
s^{\prime}u\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}s^{\prime\prime}u
because
s^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}s^{\prime\prime}
and the statement follows from the
i.h.

•

Left of a substitution
, that is,
t=s[y{\shortleftarrow}u]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]=t^{\prime}
with
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}
. Cases of
s^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}t^{\prime\prime}
:

(a)

Step in
s^{\prime}
. Then it follows from the
i.h.

(b)

Step at the root
. That is
s^{\prime}=N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
for some
N
. Then
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
and by backward deformation of CbN contexts (Lemma
52
)
s=N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
for some
N^{\prime}
such that
N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
. The we have the following diagram:
N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}y\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}
\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}

Lemma 54

(Backward deformation of CbS contexts)
.

If
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
then
t=Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
for some CbS context
Y^{\prime}
such that
Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
for every
s
.

Proof.

By induction on
t
.
Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
:

•

Root
, that is,
t=u[y{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle u\rangle
with
y\notin\mathtt{fv}(u)
. Cases of
Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=S\langle u\rangle
:

1.

Hole of
Y
in
u
, that is,
u=Y^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
and
Y=S\langle Y^{\prime\prime}\rangle
. Then
Y^{\prime}:=Y^{\prime\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]
which is indeed a silly context. Moreover,

Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=Y^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}S\langle v\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S\langle Y^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\rangle=Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}

2.

Hole of
Y
in
S
, that is,
u
is an answer,
S=S^{\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime}[z{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}]\rangle
, and
Y=S^{\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime}\langle u\rangle[z{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime\prime}]\rangle
. Then
Y^{\prime}:=u[y{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle[z{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime\prime}]\rangle]
, which verifies:

Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=u[y{\shortleftarrow}S^{\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime}\langle v\rangle[z{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}]\rangle]\mapsto_{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}S^{\prime\prime}\langle S^{\prime}\langle u\rangle[z{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}]\rangle=Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}

•

Left of an application
, that is,
t=su\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}s^{\prime}u=t^{\prime}
with
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}s^{\prime}
(because the silly context can only be a CbN context on the left of an application,
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}
steps are defined in Def.
50
, page
50
). Then
Y=Nu
. Then we have
s\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
and the statement follows from the backward deformation of CbN contexts (Lemma
52
).

•

Left of a substitution
, that is,
t=r[y{\shortleftarrow}u]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}r^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]=t^{\prime}
with
r\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}r^{\prime}
.
Cases of
Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=r^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}u]
:

1.

Hole of
Y
in
u
, that is,
r^{\prime}
is an answer,
u=Y^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
, and
Y=r^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime\prime}]
. By Lemma
49
,
r
is an answer and
Y^{\prime}:=r[y{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime\prime}]
is indeed a silly context. Moreover,

Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=r[y{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}r^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}]=Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}s\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}

2.

Hole of
Y
in
r^{\prime}
. Then it follows from the
i.h.

•

Right of a substitution
, that is,
t=u[y{\shortleftarrow}r]\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}u[y{\shortleftarrow}r^{\prime}]=t^{\prime}
with
r\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}r^{\prime}
and
u
is an answer.
Cases of
Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=u[y{\shortleftarrow}r^{\prime}]
:

1.

Hole of
Y
in
u
. Impossible, because
u
is an answer and so
x\notin\mathtt{shfv}(u)
by Lemma
32
.

2.

Hole of
Y
in
r^{\prime}
. Then it follows from the
i.h.
∎

Lemma 55

(Local postponement of silly GC by value)
.

1.

Silly multiplicative
: if

then
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{m}}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}t^{\prime\prime}

: if

: if

S\langle s\rangle=S\langle C\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\rangle\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{AY}}}S\langle C\langle u\rangle\rangle=t^{\prime\prime}
. Impossible because

cannot capture free variables of

.

•

Left of an application
, that is,

with

Left of a substitution

3.

Silly exponential 2
. Let

, that is,

.
