\gettoappendix

prop:silly-gc-postponement

Proof.

The statement is an instance of a well-known rewriting property: the local swaps in Lemma
55
above can be put together as the following
linear
local postponement property (note the
single
step postponed after
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}
):

t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}t^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}t^{\prime\prime}
then
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\neg\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{gc}\mathtt{v}}}t^{\prime\prime}
for some
t^{\prime\prime\prime}

giving the global postponement of the statement when iterated on a reduction sequence.
∎

Appendix F
Proofs of Sect.
9
(Tight Derivations, Exact Lengths, and Maximality)

\gettoappendix

prop:silly-normal-forms-forall

Proof.

By induction on
a
. Cases:

•

Base
, i.e.
a=\lambda x.t
. Then
\pi
can only be:

{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{\lambda x.t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

which satisfies the statement.

•

Inductive
, i.e.
a=a^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}a^{\prime\prime}]
. Then
\pi
has the following shape:

\pi_{a^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{a^{\prime}},e_{a^{\prime}})}{a^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{a^{\prime\prime}},e_{a^{\prime\prime}})}{a^{\prime\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a}(x)\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{a^{\prime}}+m_{a^{\prime\prime}},e_{a^{\prime}}+e_{a^{\prime\prime}})}{a[x{\shortleftarrow}a^{\prime\prime}]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}}
,
m=m_{a^{\prime}}+m_{a^{\prime\prime}}
, and
e=e_{a^{\prime}}+e_{a^{\prime\prime}}
. By
i.h.
, we have that
\pi_{a^{\prime}}
actually has shape
\pi_{a^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
. Thus, more precisely,
\pi
has the following shape:

\pi_{a^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

\pi_{a^{\prime\prime}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{a^{\prime\prime}},e_{a^{\prime\prime}})}{a^{\prime\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

\mathsf{many}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{a^{\prime\prime}},e_{a^{\prime\prime}})}{a^{\prime\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{a^{\prime\prime}},e_{a^{\prime\prime}})}{a[x{\shortleftarrow}a^{\prime\prime}]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a^{\prime\prime}}
,
m=m_{a^{\prime\prime}}
, and
e=e_{a^{\prime\prime}}
. By
i.h.
, we have that
\pi_{a^{\prime\prime}}
actually has the shape
\pi_{a^{\prime\prime}}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}
. Then
\pi
has the following shape, which satisfies the statement:

\pi_{a^{\prime}}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

\pi_{a^{\prime\prime}}\,\triangleright{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

\mathsf{many}

{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a^{\prime\prime}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,0)}{a[x{\shortleftarrow}a^{\prime\prime}]}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}}

∎

Lemma 56

(Auxiliary linear substitution on CbN contexts)
.

If
\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m,e)}{N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

then there is a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
such that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}
with
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})
there is a derivation

\pi^{\prime}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m+m^{\prime},e+e^{\prime}-1)}{N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\pi
. Cases of
N
:

•

Empty context
, i.e.
N=\langle\cdot\rangle
. The typing derivation
\pi
is simply:

{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,1)}{x}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}
is empty. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
is empty. The statement then holds with respect to
\pi^{\prime}:=\rho
, because
m=0
, and
e=1
, which give
m+m^{\prime}=m^{\prime}
and
e+e^{\prime}-1=1+e^{\prime}-1=e^{\prime}
.

•

Left of an application
, that is,
N=N^{\prime}s
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}})}N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{x\colon({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}});({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+1,e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}
,
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}})\cup\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
,
m=m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+1
, and
e=e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
.

By
i.h.
, there exists a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}
such that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}
there exists a derivation:

\pi_{N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1)}{N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

By applying a
@
rule, we obtain:

{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1)}{N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{x\colon({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}});({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+1,e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

By defining
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
, we obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
. By applying the equalities on the type context, the last obtained judgement is in fact:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}+1,e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

and, by applying those on the indices, we obtain
e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}=e+e^{\prime}-1
as required.

•

Left of a substitution
, i.e.
N=N^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]
. Note that
x\neq y
, because the hypothesis
N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
implies that
N
does not capture
x
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{x}\,\triangleright{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}};y\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}})}N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{x\colon({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}});({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}
,
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}})\cup\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
,
m=m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
, and
e=e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
.

Since we are working up to
\alpha
-equivalence, we can assume that
y\notin\mathtt{fv}(t)
, which implies
y\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})
by Lemma
9
.
By
i.h.
, there exists a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}
such that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}
there exists a derivation:

\pi_{N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};y\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1)}{N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}.

By applying rule
\mathsf{ES}
, we obtain:

{x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};y\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1)}{N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1)}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

By defining
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
, we obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
. By applying the equalities on the type context, the last obtained judgement is in fact:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}N^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

and, by applying those on the indices, we obtain
e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}=e+e^{\prime}-1
as required.∎

Lemma 57

(Tight linear substitution for CbS)
.

If
\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m,e)}{Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}}
is tight
then there is a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
such that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
with
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})
there is a derivation

\pi^{\prime}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}},x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m+m^{\prime},e+e^{\prime}-1)}{Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\pi
. Cases:

1.

Linear types
, that is,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
, because by hypothesis the type derivation is tight. We shorten
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
into
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
, for conciseness. Cases of
Y
:

•

Empty context
, i.e.
Y=\langle\cdot\rangle
. The typing derivation
\pi
is simply:

{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(0,1)}{x}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}
is empty. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
is empty. The statement then holds with respect to
\pi^{\prime}:=\rho
, because
m=0
, and
e=1
, which give
m+m^{\prime}=m^{\prime}
and
e+e^{\prime}-1=1+e^{\prime}-1=e^{\prime}
.

•

Left of an application
, that is,
Y=Ns
. Then it follows from the linear substitution lemma for the CbN case (Lemma
56
). Note that
N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
does not have type
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
, but Lemma
56
is valid for any linear type, not just
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
.

•

Left of a substitution
, i.e.
Y=Y^{\prime}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]
. Note that
x\neq y
, because the hypothesis
Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
implies that
Y
does not capture
x
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{x}\,\triangleright{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}};y\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}})}Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{x\colon({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}});({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}
,
x\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}})\cup\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}})
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
,
m=m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
, and
e=e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
.

Since we are working up to
\alpha
-equivalence, we can assume that
y\notin\mathtt{fv}(t)
, which implies
y\notin\mathtt{dom}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})
by Lemma
9
.
By
i.h.
, there exists a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}
such that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
there exists a derivation:

\pi_{Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};y\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1)}{Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}.

By applying rule
\mathsf{ES}
, we obtain:

{x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime};y\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1)}{Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1)}s\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{x\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

By defining
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}
, we obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}^{\prime}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
. By applying the equalities on the type context, the last obtained judgement is in fact:

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}})}\vdash^{(m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+m^{\prime}+m_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}},e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}})}Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[y{\shortleftarrow}s]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

and, by applying those on the indices, we obtain
e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Pi}}}+e^{\prime}-1+e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Sigma}}}=e+e^{\prime}-1
as required.

•

Right of a substitution
, that is,
Y=a[y{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime}]
. Then, the last rule of
\pi
is
\mathsf{ES}
, the left premise of which has final judgement
{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{a},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a}}\vdash^{(m_{a},e_{a})}a\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
. By Prop.
27
, we have that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a}
is empty,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}_{a}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
, and
m_{a}=0=e_{a}
, so that
\pi
actually has the following shape:

{}\vdash^{(0,0)}a\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}a[y{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

Note that the right premise now is tight, so that we can apply the
i.h.
By
i.h.
, there exists a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
such that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
there exists a derivation:

\pi_{Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m+m^{\prime},e+e^{\prime}-1)}{Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}.

By applying rule
\mathsf{ES}
, we obtain:

{}\vdash^{(0,0)}a\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m,e+e^{\prime}-1)}Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m,e+e^{\prime}-1)}a[y{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

2.

Multi types
, that is,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{T}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}
. The last rule is necessarily
\mathsf{many}
and
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}\,\triangleright{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}

\mathsf{many}

{x\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

Note that
\pi_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}
is tight. Therefore, we can apply the
i.h.
obtaining that for every derivation
\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
there exists a splitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
and a derivation:

\pi_{Y^{\prime}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m+m^{\prime},e+e^{\prime}-1)}{Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}.

By applying rule
\mathsf{many}
, we obtain:

\pi^{\prime}:=\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 13.57822pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{ ${x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m+m^{\prime},e+e^{\prime}-1)}{Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}}$ }\hskip 4.0pt}\vskip-0.69258pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to170.20006pt{\hrule\hfill}\lower 0.74812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathsf{many}$}}\vskip-0.69257pt\hbox{\hskip 1.49307pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{ ${x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m+m^{\prime},e+e^{\prime}-1))}Y\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}t\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{n}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}$ }\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces

∎

Proposition 58

(Auxiliary subject reduction for non-erasing CbN steps)
.

Let
\pi\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m,e)}{t}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
be a derivation.

1.

Multiplicative
:
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}s
then
m\geq 1
and
there is

\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m-1,e)}{s}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
.

2.

Exponential
:
if
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}s
then
e\geq 1
and
there is

\rho\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m,e-1)}{s}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}
.

Proof.

1.

Multiplicative
. Cases of
t\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}s
:

(a)

Root step
, that is,
t=S\langle\lambda x.u\rangle r\mapsto_{\mathtt{m}}S\langle u[x{\shortleftarrow}r]\rangle=s
. The proof is exactly as in the weak case (Prop.
10
, proof at page
C
), because in the root case the
m
index always decreases of exactly 1.

(b)

Left of an application
, that is,
t=ur\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}u^{\prime}r=s
with
u\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{m}}u^{\prime}
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{r}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\vdash^{(m_{r},e_{r})}r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m_{r}+1,e_{u}+e_{r})}ur\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}
,
m=m_{u}+m_{r}+1
, and
e=e_{u}+e_{r}
.

By
i.h.
,
m_{u}\geq 1
and there exists a derivation
\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash^{(m-1,e_{u})}N^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
, thus allowing us to construct
\rho
as follows:

\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash^{(m_{u}-1,e_{u})}N^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{r}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\vdash^{(m_{r},e_{r})}r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{r}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m_{r},e_{u}+e_{r})}N^{\prime}\langle r\rangle r\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

(c)

Left of a substitution
, that is,
N=N^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}p]
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u},x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}N^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{p}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m_{p},e_{u}+e_{p})}N^{\prime}\langle u\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}p]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}
,
m=m_{u}+m_{p}
, and
e=e_{u}+e_{p}
.

By
i.h.
,
m_{u}\geq 1
, and so
m\geq 1
, and there exists a derivation
\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u},x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash^{(m_{u}-1,e_{u})}N^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
, thus allowing us to construct
\rho
as follows:

\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u},x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash^{(m_{u}-1,e_{u})}N^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

\pi_{p}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{u}-1+m_{p},e_{u}+e_{p})}N^{\prime}\langle r\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}p]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

2.

Cases:

•

Root step
, i.e.
t=N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\mapsto_{\mathtt{e}_{N}}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]
. Given a derivation
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}^{\prime}}\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}^{\prime}(x)\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
by Lemma
9
, which can be applied because
x\in\mathtt{shfv}(N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}})
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{N},x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash^{(m_{N},e_{N})}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}u\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{N}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m_{N}+m_{u},e_{N}+e_{u})}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{N}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}
,
m=m_{N}+m_{u}
, and
e=e_{N}+e_{u}
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
be the splitting of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}
given by the CbN linear substitution lemma (Lemma
56
) applied to
\pi_{N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}
. By the multi-sets splitting lemma (Lemma
38
) applied to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}
, there exist two derivations:

(a)

\rho_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}},e_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}})}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}
, having as last rule a
\mathsf{many}
rule of premise
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}},e_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}})}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}
, and

(b)

\rho_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},e_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}})}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}
.

such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
,
m_{u}=m_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}+m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}
, and
e_{u}=e_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}+e_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}
.

Now, by applying again the linear substitution lemma to
\pi_{N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}x\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}
with respect to
\rho_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}
, we obtain a derivation:

\pi_{N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\,\triangleright{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{N}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{N}+m_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}},e_{N}+e_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}-1)}{N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

Then
\rho
is built as follows:

{x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}};{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{N}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{N}+m_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}},e_{N}+e_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}-1)}{N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\ {\boldsymbol{\vdash}}^{(m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},e_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}})}{u}\ {:}\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{N}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}}\vdash^{(m_{N}+m_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}+m_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}},e_{N}+e_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}}+e_{u}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{O}}-1)}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

Now, note that the last judgement is in fact

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{N}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Delta}}}\vdash^{(m_{N}+m_{u},e_{N}+e_{u}-1)}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

which in turn is

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e-1)}N\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}u\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.72221pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}[x{\shortleftarrow}u]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{L}}

as required.

•

Contextual closure.
As in the
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{m}}
case, the only change is that one looks at the
e
index rather than the
m
one. Note that indeed those cases do not depend on the details of the step itself, but only on the context enclosing it. ∎

\gettoappendix

prop:silly-subject-reduction

Proof.

1.

Multiplicative
. We have
t=Y\langle u\rangle\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}{\mathtt{m}}}Y\langle r\rangle=s
. Cases of
Y
:

•

Root step
, that is,
Y=\langle\cdot\rangle
and
t=S\langle\lambda x.u\rangle r\mapsto_{\mathtt{m}}S\langle u[x{\shortleftarrow}r]\rangle=s
. The proof is exactly as in the weak case (Prop.
10
, proof at page
C
), because in the root case the
m
index always decreases of exactly 1.

•

Left of an application
, that is,
Y=Np
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}N\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\rightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\pi_{p}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

@

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m_{p}+1,e_{u}+e_{p})}N\langle u\rangle p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}
,
m=m_{u}+m_{p}+1
, and
e=e_{u}+e_{p}
. The statement then follows by CbN subject reduction (Prop.
58
).

•

Left of a substitution
, that is,
Y=Y^{\prime}[x{\shortleftarrow}p]
. Then
\pi
has the following form:

\pi_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u},x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}Y^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\pi_{p}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m_{p},e_{u}+e_{p})}Y^{\prime}\langle u\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}p]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

With
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbf{0}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}
,
m=m_{u}+m_{p}
, and
e=e_{u}+e_{p}
.

By
i.h.
,
m_{u}\geq 1
, and so
m\geq 1
, and there exists a derivation
\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u},x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash^{(m_{u}-1,e_{u})}Y^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
, thus allowing us to construct
\rho
as follows:

\rho_{u}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u},x:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}}\vdash^{(m_{u}-1,e_{u})}Y^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\pi_{p}\,\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{p},e_{p})}p\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{M}}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{u}\uplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{p}}\vdash^{(m_{u}-1+m_{p},e_{u}+e_{p})}Y^{\prime}\langle r\rangle[x{\shortleftarrow}p]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

•

Right of a substitution
, that is,
Y=a[x{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime}]
. The last rule of
\pi
is
\mathsf{ES}
, of premise
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a}}\vdash^{(m_{a},e_{a})}a\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
.

By Prop.
27
, we have that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}_{a}
is empty and
m_{a}=0=e_{a}
, so that
\pi
actually has the following shape:

{}\vdash^{(0,0)}a\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}Y^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}Y^{\prime}\langle u\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m,e)}a[x{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime}\langle u\rangle]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

By
i.h.
, there is a derivation of final judgement
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m-1,e)}Y^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}
, and we obtain the following derivation:

{}\vdash^{(0,0)}a\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m-1,e)}Y^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

\mathsf{many}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m-1,e)}Y^{\prime}\langle r\rangle\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{[}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{]}}

\mathsf{ES}

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\Gamma}}}\vdash^{(m-1,e)}a[x{\shortleftarrow}Y^{\prime}\langle r\rangle]\,{:}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathtt{norm}}}

2.

Exponential
. Cases:

•

Root step of
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{YN}}}
, i.e.
t\mapsto_{\mathtt{e}_{N}}s
. Then it follows from CbN subject reduction (Prop.
58
).

•

Root step of
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{y}\mathtt{e}_{\mathtt{AY}}}
, i.e.
t\mapsto_{\mathtt{e}_{Y}}s
. It goes exactly as the root case for
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{n}\mathtt{e}}
for CbN subject reduction (Prop.
58
), except that one applies the right linear substitution lemma (Lemma
57
) instead of the CbN one.

•

Contextual closure.
As in the
\rightarrow_{\mathtt{m}}
case, the only change is that one looks at the
e
index rather than the
m
one. Note that indeed those cases do not depend on the details of the step itself, but only on the context enclosing it. ∎
